* **Register name** – the datasheet abbreviation is `EXTI_IMR`; for the peripheral instance `EXTI` the concrete name is `EXTI_IMR`.

  * `0` → “Interrupt request from Line x is masked”
  * `1` → “Interrupt request from Line x is not masked”

No address‑calculation formula is needed, so no function calls are required.

From the text I extracted:
- The register is named **EXTI_SWIER**.
- Its address offset is **0x10**.
- Its reset value is **0x0000 0000**.
- It is a 32‑bit register (standard word‑size for EXTI registers).
- Bits **31:18** are reserved.
- Bits **17:0** are defined as **SWIERx** (software interrupt on line *x*). Each bit is marked **rw** in the table, meaning it can be read and written. Writing a ‘1’ generates a software interrupt request (if the line is enabled in EXTI_IMR); writing a ‘0’ has no effect. The bits are cleared by writing a ‘1’ to the corresponding bit in the pending register (EXTI_PR).

From the datasheet sections titled *8.3.4 Falling trigger selection register (EXTI_FTSR)* we obtain:

* **Register abbreviation** – the datasheet names it `EXTI_FTSR`.  
* **Address offset** – listed as `0x0C`.  
* **Reset value** – listed as `0x0000 0000`.  
* **Size** – EXTI registers are 32‑bit words, so the size is 32 bits.  
* **Sub‑fields** – bits 31‑18 are Reserved (read‑only) and must stay at reset value.  
  Bits 17‑0 are individual trigger bits `TRx` (x = 0…17). Each `TRx` is a read‑write bit that enables or disables the falling‑edge trigger for line x:
  * `0` = Falling trigger disabled  
  * `1` = Falling trigger enabled  

Thus the register consists of 18 read‑write sub‑fields, one per line, each occupying a single bit.

---exti_pr---
The datasheet sections describe the **EXTI pending register (EXTI_PR)**.  
- The register name in the datasheet is `EXTI_PR`, matching the requested peripheral `exti` and register name `pr`.  
- Its address offset is explicitly given as `0x14`.  
- The reset value is stated as **undefined**, so we return `null`.  
- The register is a 32‑bit word (standard width for all EXTI registers).  
- Bits 31:18 are reserved and must be kept at their reset value.  
- Bits 0‑17 are the pending bits `PRx`. Each bit can be read to know the pending status (0 = no request, 1 = request pending) and cleared by writing a ‘1’ (write‑one‑to‑clear). This is modeled as a read‑write field covering bits 0‑17 with the enumerated values described.

**Reasoning**

- The register name for bank 2 is **FSMC_BWTR2** (abbreviation: `FSMC_BWTR2`).

- Using the provided helper function with the parameters the user supplied (`base_address_in_hex = 0xA0000000`, `start_register_number = 1`, `register_number = 2`, `register_size_in_bytes = 8`) yields the address **0xA0000008**. Interpreted as an offset from the FSMC peripheral base (`0xA0000000`), the offset is **0x0008**.  
- The reset value is stated as `0x0FFF FFFF`.  
- The register is 32 bits wide.

---fsmc_bwtr1---

**What the datasheet tells us**

* **Register abbreviation** – The datasheet names the write‑timing registers *FSMC_BWTRx* (x = 1…4). For x = 1 the abbreviation is **FSMC_BWTR1**.
* **Address offset** – The offset is given as  

  `0xA0000000 + 0x104 + 8 · (x‑1),  x = 1…4`  

  For x = 1 this evaluates to `0xA0000104`. The address offset was confirmed by the `calculate_address_offset` helper function.
* **Reset value** – The register reset value is **0x0FFF FFFF**.
* **Size** – All FSMC timing registers are 32‑bit wide (accessed as words).
* **Sub‑fields** – Table 102 (and the accompanying text) defines the bit‑fields:

| Bits | Name   | Description | Access | Enumerated values (when given) |
|------|--------|-------------|--------|--------------------------------|
|31‑30 | –      | Reserved (must be kept at reset value) | read‑only | – |
|29‑28 | ACCMOD | Access mode (asynchronous) | read‑write | 00 = Mode A, 01 = Mode B, 10 = Mode C, 11 = Mode D |
|27‑24 | DATLAT | Data latency (don’t‑care for write timing) | read‑write | – |
|23‑20 | CLKDIV | Clock‑divide ratio (don’t‑care for write timing) | read‑write | – |
|19‑16 | BUSTURN| Bus‑turnaround phase duration | read‑write | 0x0‑0xF → 0 … 15 HCLK cycles |
|15‑8  | DATAST | Data‑phase duration (for write accesses the effective duration is DATAST + 1) | read‑write | 0x00‑0xFF → 0 … 255 HCLK cycles |
|7‑4   | ADDHLD | Address‑hold phase duration (don’t‑care for write timing) | read‑write | – |
|3‑0   | ADDSET | Address‑setup phase duration | read‑write | 0x0‑0xF → 0 … 15 HCLK cycles |

All non‑reserved fields are **read‑write**.

---gpioa_crh---
**Reasoning**  
The register requested is “crh” of the GPIOA peripheral, i.e., GPIOA_CRH.  
From the extracted datasheet sections (7.2.2 Port configuration register high (GPIOx_CRH)) we have:

* Register abbreviation: GPIOA_CRH (the generic form is GPIOx_CRH, with x = A).  
* Address offset: 0x04.  
* Reset value: 0x4444 4444.  
* Register size: 32 bits (standard GPIO registers are 32‑bit words).  
* The register contains, for each pin y = 8…15, two 2‑bit fields: MODEy[1:0] and CNFy[1:0]. Both fields are read‑write.  

The enumerated values for the fields are given in the description:

* **MODEy[1:0]** – Port mode bits  
  * 00 : Input mode (reset state)  
  * 01 : Output mode, max speed 10 MHz  
  * 10 : Output mode, max speed 2 MHz  
  * 11 : Output mode, max speed 50 MHz  

* **CNFy[1:0]** – Port configuration bits, value depends on the selected mode  

  * *If MODE = 00 (input)*  
    * 00 : Analog mode  
    * 01 : Floating input (reset)  
    * 10 : Input with pull‑up / pull‑down  
    * 11 : Reserved  

  * *If MODE ≠ 00 (output)*  
    * 00 : General‑purpose output push‑pull  
    * 01 : General‑purpose output open‑drain  
    * 10 : Alternate function output push‑pull  
    * 11 : Alternate function output open‑drain  

The bit positions for each field are:

| Pin (y) | MODEy bits | CNFy bits |
|---------|------------|-----------|
| 15      | 29‑28      | 31‑30 |
| 14      | 25‑24      | 27‑26 |
| 13      | 21‑20      | 23‑22 |
| 12      | 17‑16      | 19‑18 |
| 11      | 13‑12      | 15‑14 |
| 10      | 9‑8        | 11‑10 |
| 9       | 5‑4        | 7‑6 |
| 8       | 1‑0        | 3‑2 |

All fields are **read‑write**.

No address‑calculation function is required because the offset is explicitly given.

---gpioa_bsrr---
I examined the provided excerpt of the STM32F1 reference manual. It contains the section titled **“Port bit set/reset register (GPIOx_BSRR) (x=A..G)”** which defines the register we are interested in (GPIOA_BSRR). From that section I extracted the following details:

* The register’s address offset is **0x10** and its reset value is **0x0000 0000**.  
* The register is a **32‑bit** word‑accessed register.  
* Bits **15:0** are the **BSy** (set) bits, one for each port line y = 0…15. These bits are **write‑only**; writing a ‘1’ sets the corresponding ODR bit, ‘0’ has no effect.  
* Bits **31:16** are the **BRy** (reset) bits, one for each port line y = 0…15. These bits are also **write‑only**; writing a ‘1’ resets the corresponding ODR bit, ‘0’ has no effect.  

Thus the register consists of 32 sub‑fields, each 1 bit wide, with the same enumerated values (0 = “No action”, 1 = “Set” or “Reset” depending on the field).

No calculation of the address offset is required because the offset is given explicitly.

---dbg_idcode---

The relevant sections describe the **DBGMCU IDCODE** register, which is part of the DBGMCU component. It is located at the fixed address 0xE004 2000, is a 32‑bit read‑only register, and its fields are defined as:

The datasheet does not give an explicit reset value for the whole register; it only shows that the fields are “X” (unknown/implementation‑defined) after reset. Therefore the reset value field will be left as `null`.

* **Sub‑fields** are identified in the bit‑field table:
  * **EWUP** – bit 8, read‑write, enables the WKUP pin. Enumerated values: 0 = WKUP pin is general‑purpose I/O; 1 = WKUP pin enabled for wake‑up.
  * **PVDO** – bit 2, read‑only, PVD output. Enumerated values: 0 = VDD/VDDA > threshold; 1 = VDD/VDDA < threshold.
  * **SBF** – bit 1, read‑only, Standby flag. Enumerated values: 0 = Device has not been in Standby; 1 = Device has been in Standby.
  * **WUF** – bit 0, read‑only, Wake‑up flag. Enumerated values: 0 = No wake‑up event; 1 = Wake‑up event occurred.

All other bits (31:9 and 7:3) are reserved and are omitted from the sub‑field list.

|9‑8   | CC2S[1:0] | Capture/Compare 2 selection (direction/input) |
|7     | Reserved | – |
|6‑4   | OC1M[2:0] | Output compare 1 mode |
|3     | OC1PE | Output compare 1 preload enable |
|2     | OC1FE | Output compare 1 fast enable |
|1‑0   | CC1S[1:0] | Capture/Compare 1 selection (direction/input) |

All listed fields are read‑write (reserved bits are read‑only).  
Enumerated values are defined for the OCxM fields (000‑111) as described in the text.

No address‑calculation function is required because the offset is explicitly given.

  * **MSM** – bit 7, read‑write, with two enumerated values (0 = No action, 1 = Master/Slave mode enabled).

---tim2_ccmr1_input---
**Reasoning:**  
The requested register is the input‑mode view of the TIMx capture/compare mode register 1 for TIM2, i.e. **TIM2_CCMR1**.  
The datasheet section “13.4.7 TIMx capture/compare mode register 1 (TIMx_CCMR1)” gives:

* **Address offset:** 0x18  
* **Reset value:** 0x0000  
* **Size:** 16 bits (bits 15…0 are defined).  

The register contains fields that are used when the channel operates in input (capture) mode. Relevant sub‑fields are:

| Bits | Name | Description | Access |
|------|------|-------------|--------|
| 1:0  | CC1S | Capture/Compare 1 selection – defines direction and which input is mapped to channel 1. | read‑write (writable only when channel 1 is OFF) |
| 3:2  | IC1PSC | Input capture 1 prescaler – division ratio for the captured input. | read‑write |
| 7:4  | IC1F | Input capture 1 filter – digital filter configuration for TI1. | read‑write |
| 9:8  | CC2S | Capture/Compare 2 selection – defines direction and which input is mapped to channel 2. | read‑write (writable only when channel 2 is OFF) |
| 11:10| IC2PSC| Input capture 2 prescaler – division ratio for the captured input. | read‑write |
| 15:12| IC2F | Input capture 2 filter – digital filter configuration for TI2. | read‑write |

Enumerated values are provided for the CCxS fields:

* **00** – Channel configured as output.  
* **01** – Channel configured as input, ICx mapped on TIx.  
* **10** – Channel configured as input, ICx mapped on TIy (the other TI line).  
* **11** – Channel configured as input, ICx mapped on TRC (internal trigger).  

No other information (e.g., address formula) requires a function call.

**Output JSON** follows the required schema.

---tim2_dmar---
The datasheet sections for general‑purpose timers (TIM2‑TIM5) contain a description of the **TIMx_DMAR** register.  
For TIM2 the register name becomes **TIM2_DMAR**.  

* **Address offset** is explicitly given as **0x4C**.  
* **Reset value** is stated as **0x0000**.  
* The register is a 16‑bit register (all TIM2‑TIM5 registers are 16‑bit addressable).  
* The register bit‑field layout is:

| Bits | Name | Description | Access |
|------|------|-------------|--------|
|15:13|Reserved|Must be kept at reset value|read‑only (treated as reserved)|
|12:8|**DBL[4:0]**|DMA burst length – number of transfers (0 = 1 transfer, … 0x11 = 18 transfers)|read‑write|
|7:5|Reserved|Must be kept at reset value|read‑only|
|4:0|**DBA[4:0]**|DMA base address – offset from TIMx_CR1 (0 = TIMx_CR1, 1 = TIMx_CR2, 2 = TIMx_SMCR, …)|read‑write|
|15:0|**DMAB[15:0]**|DMA register for burst accesses (the data transferred on each DMA beat)|read‑write|

Enumerated values are provided for the **DBL** and **DBA** fields in the text (e.g., DBL = 0 → 1 transfer, DBL = 1 → 2 transfers; DBA = 0 → TIMx_CR1, DBA = 1 → TIMx_CR2, DBA = 2 → TIMx_SMCR, …).

No address‑calculation is required because the offset is given directly.

---tim2_ccmr2_output---
I examined the provided excerpts that describe the “TIMx capture/compare mode register 2 (TIMx_CCMR2)”.  
The section **13.4.8 TIMx capture/compare mode register 2 (TIMx_CCMR2)** states:

* **Address offset:** 0x1C  
* **Reset value:** 0x0000  
* The register is 32 bits wide (standard for all TIMx registers).  

The register is split into two halves, each handling one channel (channel 4 in the high half, channel 3 in the low half). For each channel the following bit‑fields are defined in **output compare mode**:

| Channel | Bit(s) | Name | Description | Access |
|---------|--------|------|-------------|--------|
| 4 | 15 | OC4CE | Output compare 4 clear enable | read‑write |
| 4 | 14‑12 | OC4M[2:0] | Output compare 4 mode (enumerated 0‑7) | read‑write |
| 4 | 11 | OC4PE | Output compare 4 preload enable | read‑write |
| 4 | 10 | OC4FE | Output compare 4 fast enable | read‑write |
| 4 | 9‑8 | CC4S[1:0] | Capture/Compare 4 selection (00 = output, 01‑11 = input configurations) | read‑write |
| 3 | 7 | OC3CE | Output compare 3 clear enable | read‑write |
| 3 | 6‑4 | OC3M[2:0] | Output compare 3 mode (enumerated 0‑7) | read‑write |
| 3 | 3 | OC3PE | Output compare 3 preload enable | read‑write |
| 3 | 2 | OC3FE | Output compare 3 fast enable | read‑write |
| 3 | 1‑0 | CC3S[1:0] | Capture/Compare 3 selection (00 = output, 01‑11 = input configurations) | read‑write |

Enumerated values for the mode fields (OCxM) follow the standard TIMx definitions (0 = Frozen, 1 = Set on match, …, 7 = PWM mode 2). The selection fields (CCxS) follow the same pattern as described for other channels (00 = output, 01 = input‑TIx, 10 = input‑TIy, 11 = input‑TRC).

No address‑offset calculation is required because the offset is given explicitly.

The bit‑field description for WWDG_CFR includes:

   - 11: … ÷ 8

* **Size** – the WWDG registers can be accessed as half‑words (16 bits) or words (32 bits). The control register only defines bits 7:0, so the effective size is 16 bits.  
* **Sub‑fields** – the register description shows two usable fields:  
  * **WDGA** – bit 7, an activation bit that can be written by software and is cleared by hardware after reset. It is therefore read‑write with two enumerated values (0 = disabled, 1 = enabled).  
  * **T[6:0]** – bits 6‑0, a 7‑bit counter value (MSB to LSB). It is readable and writable (read‑write) but has no enumerated list.

No other sub‑fields are defined (bits 31:8 are reserved and must stay at the reset value).

---iwdg_sr---
The datasheet sections titled **“18.4.4 Status register (IWDG_SR)”** describe the register we are interested in.  
- The register is named **IWDG_SR**, which matches the requested peripheral (IWDG) and register name (sr).

The bit‑field description shows that bits 31‑2 are reserved.  
Bits 1 and 0 are defined:

* **RVU (bit 1)** – “Watchdog counter reload value update”. It is set by hardware when a reload is in progress and cleared by hardware when the update completes. This makes it **read‑only**.  
* **PVU (bit 0)** – “Watchdog prescaler value update”. It is set by hardware when a prescaler change is in progress and cleared by hardware when the update completes. This is also **read‑only**.  

Both bits have two enumerated states (0 = reset, 1 = update ongoing).

No other subfields are defined, and no address calculation is needed, so no function calls are required.

---bkp_dr19---
**Reasoning**  
The requested register is `dr19` of the `bkp` peripheral, which corresponds to the backup data register **BKP_DR19**.  

From the BKP register map (Table 14) the offset for `BKP_DR19` is listed explicitly as **0x60**. The same offset can be derived using the formula for registers 11‑20:  

\[
\text{offset} = 0x40 + (x-11) \times 4
\]

For `x = 19` this yields `0x40 + (19‑11)*4 = 0x60`, which matches the explicit listing.  

All `BKP_DRx` registers have the same reset value **0x0000 0000** and are 16‑bit wide. The register contains a single read‑write field `D[15:0]` that holds the backup data. No enumerated values are defined for this field.  

The address offset has been confirmed by the `calculate_address_offset` function call, which returned `0x60`.

  * **ASOS** – bit 9, read‑write, selects whether the TAMPER pin outputs the RTC Alarm pulse (0) or the RTC Second pulse (1).  
  * **ASOE** – bit 8, read‑write, enables the alarm/second‑pulse output on the TAMPER pin.  
  * **CCO** – bit 7, read‑write, enables the calibration‑clock output (RTC clock divided by 64) on the TAMPER pin.  
  * **CAL[6:0]** – bits 6‑0, read‑write, calibration value (0‑0x7F) that determines the number of ignored RTC clock pulses.

All other bits are Reserved and therefore not listed as sub‑fields.

I examined the provided excerpts that describe the **TIMx_CCMR1** register for the TIM15/16/17 family. It also lists the bit‑field layout for the output‑compare mode of channel 1, which is the relevant mode for TIM16 (TIM16 only has channel 1). From the description I extracted the sub‑fields, their bit positions, access type (read‑write), and the enumerated values that are documented for each field. The register is a 16‑bit register.

---tim16_arr---
I looked through the provided sections of the STM32F1 reference manual (RM0041) for the TIM16 peripheral. The auto‑reload register for TIM16 (and TIM17) is described under **15.6.10 TIM16&TIM17 auto‑reload register (TIMx_ARR)**. It states:

- **Address offset:** 0x2C  
- **Reset value:** 0x0000  
- **Register layout:** a 16‑bit field ARR[15:0] that holds the auto‑reload value. The field is marked “rw”, meaning it is read‑write. No other bits are defined (the whole 16‑bit register is the ARR field).

From the naming convention TIMx_ARR with x = 16, the concrete register name for TIM16 is **TIM16_ARR**.

---tim16_rcr---
The datasheet section **15.6.11 TIM16&TIM17 repetition counter register (TIMx_RCR)** describes the register that matches the requested name **rcr** for the **TIM16** peripheral.

- The register is named **TIMx_RCR** where *x* = 16, so the concrete abbreviation is **TIM16_RCR** (or simply **TIM16_RCR** in the JSON field `datasheet_register_abbreviation`).
- The address offset is explicitly given as **0x30**.
- The reset value is explicitly given as **0x0000**.
- The register is 16 bits wide (the table shows bits 15 down to 0).
- Bits **15:8** are Reserved and must stay at the reset value, so they are not listed as a usable sub‑field.
- Bits **7:0** are defined as **REP[7:0]**, the repetition counter value. The access type is **read‑write** (shown as “rw” in the bit‑field table). No enumerated values are described for this field.

No address‑offset calculation is required because the offset is directly provided.

---tim16_ccmr1_input---

| Bit | Name | Description (summarised) | Access | Enumerated values |

| 7   | BG   | Break generation – software sets to generate a break event, cleared automatically by hardware. | write‑only | 0 = No action, 1 = Break event generated |
| 6   | TG   | Trigger generation – software sets to generate a trigger event, cleared automatically by hardware. | write‑only | 0 = No action, 1 = TIF flag set |
| 5   | COMG | Capture/Compare control update generation – software sets, cleared automatically. | write‑only | 0 = No action, 1 = Updates CCxE, CCxNE and OCxM when CCPC=1 |

---tim16_cr2---

  * **CEN** – bit 0, read‑write, with two enumerated values (0 = counter disabled, 1 = counter enabled).
  * All other bits are reserved and must be kept at their reset value, so they are not listed as sub‑fields.

---tim16_dcr---
I searched the provided excerpts for the register named **dcr** belonging to **TIM16**.

From that description I extracted:

* Its address offset is given explicitly as `0x48`.  
* The reset value is listed as `0x0000`.  
* The register is defined with bits 15‑0, so its size is 16 bits.  
* The documented sub‑fields are:
  * **DBL[4:0]** – bits 12:8 – DMA burst length, read‑write, with enumerated values that map a 5‑bit code to the number of transfers (0 → 1 transfer, 1 → 2 transfers, …, 0x11 → 18 transfers).  
  * **OSSI** – bit 10 – Off‑state selection for Idle mode, read‑write, enumerated values 0 → disabled, 1 → enabled.  
  * **DBA[4:0]** – bits 4:0 – DMA base address, read‑write, enumerated values that select the base register for DMA accesses (0 → TIMx_CR1, 1 → TIMx_CR2, 2 → TIMx_SMCR, …).

All other bits are reserved and therefore omitted from the sub‑field list.

  * **ARPE** – bit 7, read‑write, 0 = not buffered, 1 = buffered.  
  * **OPM** – bit 3, read‑write, 0 = normal mode, 1 = one‑pulse mode.  
  * **URS** – bit 2, read‑write, 0 = any event generates an update, 1 = only overflow generates an update.  
  * **UDIS** – bit 1, read‑write, 0 = update enabled, 1 = update disabled.  
  * **CEN** – bit 0, read‑write, 0 = counter disabled, 1 = counter enabled.  
  Bits 15:10 and 6:4 are reserved and therefore omitted from the sub‑field list.

No address calculation is required because the offset is directly provided.

---dac_dhr12r1---
The datasheet sections for the DAC peripheral describe the **DAC_DHR12R1** register, which matches the requested register name *dhr12r1*.  
- The register abbreviation is **DAC_DHR12R1**.  
- Its address offset is given as **0x08**.  
- The reset value is listed as **0x0000 0000**.  
- All DAC registers are word‑accessed, so the size is **32 bits**.  
- The bit‑field definition shows bits 31:12 are reserved, and bits 11:0 are the **DACC1DHR[11:0]** field that holds the 12‑bit right‑aligned data for channel 1. This field is read‑write; no enumerated values are defined for it.

---dac_dhr12l1---

- The register is part of the DAC peripheral and, like all DAC registers, is accessed as a **32‑bit word**.

Thus the required information can be extracted directly without any calculation function.

---crc_cr---
The datasheet sections for the CRC peripheral describe a **control register (CRC_CR)**.  
- Its abbreviation is `CRC_CR` (the peripheral name `CRC` plus the register suffix `CR`).  
- The address offset is explicitly given as `0x08`.  
- The reset value is given as `0x0000 0000`.  
- The register is accessed as a 32‑bit word, so its size is 32 bits.  

The register map lists two distinct fields:  

1. **Bits 7:0 – General‑purpose 8‑bit data register bits**  
   * Description: a temporary 8‑bit storage that is not affected by a CRC reset.  
   * Access: read‑write (rw).  
   * Bit range: start 0, end 7.  
   * No enumerated values.  

2. **Bit 0 – RESET bit** (overlaps the LSB of the previous field)  
   * Description: writing a ‘1’ resets the CRC calculation unit and forces the data register to `0xFFFFFFFF`; the bit clears automatically.  
   * Access: write‑only (w).  
   * Bit range: start 0, end 0.  
   * Enumerated values: 0 = “No effect”, 1 = “Reset CRC unit”.  

All other bits (31:8 or 31:1) are reserved and therefore omitted from the sub‑field list.

---dma1_cmar3---
**Reasoning**  
The register requested is **CMAR3**, i.e., the DMA channel 3 memory address register for DMA1. The datasheet section *9.4.6 DMA channel x memory address register (DMA_CMARx)* states:

* Address offset formula: `0x14 + 0d20 × (channel number – 1)`.  
  Using the provided function result for channel 3, the calculated offset is **0x1C**.  
* Reset value: `0x0000 0000`.  
* Size: All DMA_CMARx registers are 32 bits (4 bytes).  
* Subfields: The register contains a single field **MA[31:0]** (“Memory address”) that occupies bits 31‑0, is read/write (`rw`), and has no enumerated values.

Thus the extracted information is:

* **datasheet_register_abbreviation** – `DMA_CMAR3` (peripheral DMA1 + register name).  
* **address_offset** – `0x1C`.  
* **reset_value** – `0x00000000`.  
* **size** – 32 bits.  
* **subfields** – one subfield `MA` covering bits 31‑0, read‑write, with the description given above.

---dma1_cndtr3---
The datasheet specifies the **DMA channel x number of data register (DMA_CNDTRx)**.  
For channel 3 (x = 3) this corresponds to the register **cndtr3** of the DMA1 peripheral.

- **Register abbreviation** used in the reference manual: **DMA_CNDTR3**; with the peripheral instance it becomes **DMA1_CNDTR3**.  
- **Address offset** is given by the formula  
  `Address offset = 0x0C + 20 × (channel – 1)` (decimal 20 = 0x14).  
  For channel 3 the correct calculation is `0x0C + 0x14 × 2 = 0x34`.  
  To obtain this value I invoke the `calculate_address_offset` tool with a register size of 20 bytes.
- **Reset value**: `0x0000 0000`.  
- **Size**: 32 bits (bits 31‑0).  
- **Sub‑fields**:  
  * **NDT[15:0]** – “Number of data to transfer” (0 … 65535).  
    It can be written by software when the channel is disabled; when the channel is enabled it becomes read‑only, showing the remaining data count. Therefore it is treated as **read‑write**. No enumerated values are defined.

**Function calls**

---dma1_ccr2---
I searched through the provided sections of the STM32F100 reference manual for any register named **CCR2** that belongs to the **DMA1** peripheral.  

The DMA1 peripheral defines the following registers for each channel x (x = 1…7):  

* DMA_CCRx – channel‑x configuration register  
* DMA_CNDTRx – channel‑x number of data register  
* DMA_CPARx – channel‑x peripheral address register  
* DMA_CMARx – channel‑x memory address register  

Additionally, the DMA controller has global registers (ISR, IFCR, etc.). None of these registers are named **CCR2**, nor is there any description of a “CCR2” field within the DMA1 register map. The only occurrences of “CCR2” in the extracted text refer to timer capture/compare registers (TIMx_CCR2), which belong to timer peripherals, not to DMA1.

Therefore, the datasheet does not contain any information about a **ccr2** register in the **dma1** peripheral.

No JSON output can be provided because the required information is absent.

---dma1_ifcr---
The register we are asked about is **IFCR** of the **DMA1** peripheral.  
In the provided excerpts the section titled *“9.4.2 DMA interrupt flag clear register (DMA_IFCR)”* describes this register.  

Key points extracted:

* **Register abbreviation** – the datasheet names it **DMA_IFCR**.  
* **Address offset** – explicitly given as **0x04**.  
* **Reset value** – explicitly given as **0x0000 0000**.  
* **Size** – the register is shown with bits 0‑31, i.e. a **32‑bit** register.  
* **Sub‑fields** – the register contains four groups of one‑bit fields, each repeated for channels 1‑7:  

| Sub‑field | Bit position (per channel x) | Description | Access | Enumerated values |
|-----------|-----------------------------|-------------|--------|-------------------|
| **CTEIFx** | 4·(x‑1)+3 (bits 27, 23, 19, 15, 11, 7, 3) | Channel x transfer‑error clear | write‑only | 0 = No effect, 1 = Clears TEIF flag |
| **CHTIFx** | 4·(x‑1)+2 (bits 26, 22, 18, 14, 10, 6, 2) | Channel x half‑transfer clear | write‑only | 0 = No effect, 1 = Clears HTIF flag |
| **CTCIFx** | 4·(x‑1)+1 (bits 25, 21, 17, 13, 9, 5, 1) | Channel x transfer‑complete clear | write‑only | 0 = No effect, 1 = Clears TCIF flag |
| **CGIFx**  | 4·(x‑1)+0 (bits 24, 20, 16, 12, 8, 4, 0) | Channel x global‑interrupt clear | write‑only | 0 = No effect, 1 = Clears GIF, TEIF, HTIF and TCIF flags |

All bits 31‑28 are reserved and must be kept at their reset value.

No address‑calculation is required because the offset is directly provided.

---dma1_cndtr1---
**Reasoning**  
The datasheet section **9.4.4 DMA channel x number of data register (DMA_CNDTRx)** provides the details for the register we are interested in.

- **Register name**: The generic name is `DMA_CNDTRx`. For channel 1 (x = 1) this becomes `DMA_CNDTR1`.  
- **Address offset**: Given as `0x0C + 0d20 × (channel number – 1)`. Using the provided `calculate_address_offset` function with `base_address_in_hex = 0x0C`, `start_register_number = 1`, `register_number = 1`, and `register_size_in_bytes = 4` yields `0xC`.  
- **Reset value**: Explicitly stated as `0x0000 0000`.  
- **Size**: The register is 32 bits wide (bits 31 … 0 are defined).  
- **Sub‑fields**:  
  * **NDT[15:0]** (bits 15 to 0) – “Number of data to transfer”. This field can be written when the channel is disabled and read otherwise, so its access type is **read‑write**.  
  * Bits 31 to 16 are reserved and therefore omitted from the sub‑field list.  
- **Enumerated values**: None are defined for the NDT field.

The calculated address offset (`0xC`) is used directly in the JSON output.

- The register is shown with bits 15…0, so its size is 16 bits.  
- The bit‑field table lists the functional sub‑fields (CKD, ARPE, CMS, DIR, OPM, URS, UDIS, CEN) together with their descriptions and read‑write access. Enumerated values are described for each of those fields.

Thus I can construct the required JSON with the register abbreviation “TIM1_CR1”, its offset, reset value, size, and a list of sub‑fields extracted from the datasheet.

---tim1_egr---
I examined the provided excerpts and identified the section titled **“12.4.6 TIM1 event generation register (TIMx_EGR)”** which describes the TIM1 EGR register. All relevant information is present for the TIM1 peripheral:

* The register is named **TIM1_EGR** (datasheet abbreviation).
* The address offset is given as **0x14**.
* The reset value is **0x0000**.
* The register layout shows bits 0‑7 defined and bits 8‑15 reserved, indicating a **16‑bit** register.
* Bits 0‑7 are the functional sub‑fields:
  * **UG** (Update generation) – write‑only, 0 = No action, 1 = Re‑initialises the counter.
  * **CC1G** (Capture/compare 1 generation) – write‑only, 0 = No action, 1 = Generates a CC1 event.
  * **CC2G** – write‑only, same encoding as CC1G.
  * **CC3G** – write‑only, same encoding as CC1G.
  * **CC4G** – write‑only, same encoding as CC1G.
  * **COMG** (Capture/Compare control update generation) – write‑only, 0 = No action, 1 = Updates CCxE/CCxNE/OCxM when CCPC is set.
  * **TG** (Trigger generation) – write‑only, 0 = No action, 1 = Sets the TIF flag.
  * **BG** (Break generation) – write‑only, 0 = No action, 1 = Generates a break event.
* All other bits are reserved and must be kept at the reset value.

No address‑calculation formula is needed, so no function calls are required.

---adc1_jdr1---

The register is accessed as a 32‑bit word. Bits 31‑16 are marked as *Reserved* and must remain at their reset value, while bits 15‑0 are defined as **JDATA[15:0]**, which contain the conversion result from the injected channel 1. These bits are **read‑only**. No enumerated values are provided for the data field.

---adc1_cr1---
The datasheet sections titled **“10.11.2 ADC control register 1 (ADC_CR1)”** describe the register that matches the requested peripheral `adc1` and register name `cr1`.  
From the description we can extract:

* **Register abbreviation** – the datasheet calls it **ADC_CR1** (for ADC 1 this becomes ADC1_CR1).  
* **Address offset** – explicitly given as **0x04**.  
* **Reset value** – explicitly given as **0x0000 0000**.  
* **Register size** – the peripheral registers are accessed by 32‑bit words, so the size is **32 bits**.  
* **Sub‑fields** – the bits that are defined (non‑reserved) are listed with their names, a brief description, access type (all are software‑settable, i.e. read‑write) and, when appropriate, enumerated values.  
  * Bit 23 – **AWDEN** – “Analog watchdog enable on regular channels”.  
  * Bit 22 – **JAWDEN** – “Analog watchdog enable on injected channels”.  
  * Bits 15‑13 – **DISCNUM[2:0]** – “Discontinuous mode channel count”. Values 0‑7 correspond to 1‑8 channels.  
  * Bit 12 – **JDISCEN** – “Discontinuous mode on injected channels”.  
  * Bit 11 – **DISCEN** – “Discontinuous mode on regular channels”.  
  * Bit 10 – **JAUTO** – “Automatic injected group conversion”.  

All other bits are reserved and are therefore omitted from the sub‑field list.

No address‑calculation is required because the offset is given directly.

---adc1_cr2---
I examined the provided excerpts for the ADC peripheral and identified the section titled **“10.11.3 ADC control register 2 (ADC_CR2)”**.  
From this section the register’s abbreviation, address offset, reset value and size are explicitly given.  
The description enumerates several bit‑fields together with their meanings and the possible enumerated values.  
All of those bit‑fields are software‑settable, therefore their access type is *read‑write*.  
Bits that are marked as *Reserved* are omitted from the sub‑field list.

**Extracted information**

- Register abbreviation in the datasheet: **ADC_CR2** (for ADC1 this becomes ADC1_CR2).  
- Address offset: **0x08**.  
- Reset value: **0x00000000**.  
- Register size: **32 bits**.  
- Sub‑fields: AWDSGL, SCAN, JEOCIE, AWDIE, EOCIE, AWDCH[4:0], TSVREFE, SWSTART, DISCNUM[2:0], JDISCEN, DISCEN, JAUTO (the last four are described later in the same page).

---adc1_jdr2---
The datasheet excerpt for the ADC peripheral lists the **ADC_JDR2** register at address offset 0x40. It shows the register is 32 bits wide, with bits 31:16 marked as Reserved (must stay at reset value) and bits 15:0 defined as **JDATA[15:0]**. The description states these bits contain the conversion result from injected channel 2 and are **read‑only**. The reset value for the whole register is given as all zeros (0 0 … 0), i.e., 0x0000 0000. No enumerated values are provided for the JDATA field.

Thus the required information can be extracted directly without any calculations.

---adc1_jofr3---
I scanned the provided excerpts for any mention of a register named **JOFR3** (injected channel data offset register x). The ADC section lists the series of injected‑channel offset registers:

- `ADC_JOFR1` – address 0x14  
- `ADC_JOFR2` – address 0x18  
- **`ADC_JOFR3` – address 0x1C**  
- `ADC_JOFR4` – address 0x20  

For each of them the datasheet shows the same field layout: a 12‑bit field `JOFFSETx[11:0]` (bits 0‑11) and the remaining bits 31‑12 marked as **Reserved**. The reset line for `ADC_JOFR3` contains twelve “0” entries, which corresponds to a reset value of 0x0000 0000. All ADC registers are 32‑bit wide, so the size is 32 bits. The field `JOFFSET3` is marked as read‑write (`rw`). No enumerated values are defined for this field.

No address‑calculation formula is needed because the offset is given explicitly.

---adc1_jofr1---
The datasheet describes the ADC injected channel data offset registers (ADC_JOFRx) for x = 1..4.  
For **ADC_JOFR1** the following details are provided:

* **Register abbreviation** – the datasheet names it **ADC_JOFR1**.  
* **Address offset** – listed as **0x14** (the range 0x14‑0x20 covers JOFR1‑JOFR4).  
* **Reset value** – **0x0000 0000**.  
* **Size** – ADC registers are 32‑bit wide, and the bit‑field table shows bits 31…0.  
* **Sub‑fields** – bits 11:0 are the field **JOFFSET1[11:0]** that holds the data offset for injected channel 1. It is described as “Data offset for injected channel x” and is **read‑write**. Bits 31:12 are reserved and therefore omitted from the sub‑field list.

No calculation of the address offset is required because the exact offset is explicitly given.

---adc1_smpr2---
The datasheet sections for **ADC sample time register 2 (ADC_SMPR2)** clearly state:

* The register belongs to the ADC peripheral, so for ADC1 the full name is **ADC_SMPR2**.  
* **Address offset** is given as **0x10**.  
* **Reset value** is **0x0000 0000**.  
* All ADC registers are 32 bits wide, therefore the **size** is 32 bits.  
* The register contains the sample‑time selection fields **SMP0 … SMP9** (each 3 bits, read‑write). Bits 31‑30 are reserved.  
* For each SMPx field the enumerated values are the same:
  * 000 → 1.5 cycles  
  * 001 → 7.5 cycles  
  * 010 → 13.5 cycles  
  * 011 → 28.5 cycles  
  * 100 → 41.5 cycles  
  * 101 → 55.5 cycles  
  * 110 → 71.5 cycles  
  * 111 → 239.5 cycles  

From the bit‑field tables we can derive the bit positions:
* SMP0 occupies bits 2 ‑ 0, SMP1 bits 5 ‑ 3, …, SMP9 bits 29 ‑ 27.  

All these fields are **read‑write**.

No address‑calculation function is required because the offset is given directly.

---adc1_sr---
I examined the provided excerpts and located the section that describes the ADC status register for the ADC peripheral. The register is listed in the ADC register map as `ADC_SR` (at offset 0x00) and is the status register for ADC 1, matching the requested peripheral `adc1`. The table shows a reset value of all zeros, confirming a reset value of 0x00000000. The register is 32 bits wide. The status bits are the standard ADC status flags (analog‑watchdog, end‑of‑conversion, injected‑end‑of‑conversion, injected‑start, regular‑start, overrun) occupying bits 5 down to 0, each being read‑only and having enumerated values 0 (“no event”) and 1 (“event occurred”).
